\doxysubsection{詳解}
\subsubsection*{template$<$typename Derived$>$\newline
class Eigen\+::\+Dense\+Base$<$ Derived $>$}
Base class for all dense matrices, vectors, and arrays 

This class is the base that is inherited by all dense objects (matrix, vector, arrays, and related expression types). The common \doxylink{namespace_eigen}{Eigen} API for dense objects is contained in this class.


\begin{DoxyTemplParams}{テンプレート引数}
{\em Derived} & is the derived type, e.\+g., a matrix type or an expression.\\
\hline
\end{DoxyTemplParams}
This class can be extended with the help of the plugin mechanism described on the page \doxylink{TopicCustomizing_Plugins}{Extending Matrix\+Base (and other classes)} by defining the preprocessor symbol {\ttfamily EIGEN\+\_\+\+DENSEBASE\+\_\+\+PLUGIN}.

\begin{DoxySeeAlso}{参照}
\textbackslash{}blank \doxylink{TopicClassHierarchy}{The class hierarchy} 
\end{DoxySeeAlso}

\raisebox{\heightof{T}}[0pt][0pt]{\index{MaxRowsAtCompileTime@{MaxRowsAtCompileTime}!Eigen::DenseBase$<$ Derived $>$@{Eigen::DenseBase$<$ Derived $>$}}\index{Eigen::DenseBase$<$ Derived $>$@{Eigen::DenseBase$<$ Derived $>$}!MaxRowsAtCompileTime@{MaxRowsAtCompileTime}}}\Hypertarget{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a9830d779ed6ffc4e370c5ce089edd205_a9830d779ed6ffc4e370c5ce089edd205ad2baadea085372837b0e80dc93be1306}\label{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a9830d779ed6ffc4e370c5ce089edd205_a9830d779ed6ffc4e370c5ce089edd205} 
Max\+Rows\+At\+Compile\+Time&This value is equal to the maximum possible number of rows that this expression might have. If this expression might have an arbitrarily high number of rows, this value is set to {\itshape Dynamic}.

This value is useful to know when evaluating an expression, in order to determine whether it is possible to avoid doing a dynamic memory allocation.

This function is mainly useful for blocks where the number of rows is specified at compile-\/time and the number of columns is specified at run-\/time, or vice versa. The compile-\/time and run-\/time information should not contradict. In other words, {\itshape block\+Rows} should equal {\itshape NRows} unless {\itshape NRows} is {\itshape Dynamic}, and the same for the number of columns.

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+: 
\begin{DoxyVerbInclude}
\end{DoxyVerbInclude}


\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}

\textbackslash{}newin\{3.\+4\}\+:

The number of rows {\itshape block\+Rows} and columns {\itshape block\+Cols} can also be specified at compile-\/time by passing \doxylink{namespace_eigen_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418}{Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$}, or Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$(n) as arguments. In the later case, {\ttfamily n} plays the role of a runtime fallback value in case {\ttfamily N} equals \doxylink{namespace_eigen_ad81fa7195215a0ce30017dfac309f0b2_ad81fa7195215a0ce30017dfac309f0b2}{Eigen\+::\+Dynamic}. Here is an example with a fixed number of rows {\ttfamily NRows} and dynamic number of columns {\ttfamily cols\+:} 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_tutorial___advanced_initialization___comma_temporary_8cpp_a5d2ded7a4c8830608d7b218f5f27c0a0_a5d2ded7a4c8830608d7b218f5f27c0a0}{mat}}.block(\mbox{\hyperlink{_bi_c_g_s_t_a_b__step__by__step_8cpp_acb559820d9ca11295b4500f179ef6392_acb559820d9ca11295b4500f179ef6392}{i}},\mbox{\hyperlink{tut__arithmetic__redux__minmax_8cpp_a39513f0f8d942bf9ef17b2e87ac6d34e_a39513f0f8d942bf9ef17b2e87ac6d34e}{j}},fix<NRows>,\mbox{\hyperlink{_tutorial__commainit__02_8cpp_a4407a60bc4387adae24cee658711f2d9_a4407a60bc4387adae24cee658711f2d9}{cols}})}

\end{DoxyCode}


This function thus fully covers the features offered by the following overloads \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a20ca095fbf288a393066b0e08a504280_a20ca095fbf288a393066b0e08a504280}{block$<$\+NRows,\+NCols$>$(\+Index, Index)}, and \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_adf40a0b66634af3002950c915ed15950_adf40a0b66634af3002950c915ed15950}{block$<$\+NRows,\+NCols$>$(\+Index, Index, Index, Index)} that are thus obsolete. Indeed, this generic version avoids redundancy, it preserves the argument order, and prevents the need to rely on the template keyword in templated code.

but with less redundancy and more consistency as it does not modify the argument order and seamlessly enable hybrid fixed/dynamic sizes.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
Even in the case that the returned expression has dynamic size, in the case when it is applied to a fixed-\/size matrix, it inherits a fixed maximal size, which means that evaluating it does not cause a dynamic memory allocation. 
\end{DoxyNote}

This function is mainly useful for corners where the number of rows is specified at compile-\/time and the number of columns is specified at run-\/time, or vice versa. The compile-\/time and run-\/time information should not contradict. In other words, {\itshape c\+Rows} should equal {\itshape CRows} unless {\itshape CRows} is {\itshape Dynamic}, and the same for the number of columns.

This function is mainly useful for corners where the number of rows is specified at compile-\/time and the number of columns is specified at run-\/time, or vice versa. The compile-\/time and run-\/time information should not contradict. In other words, {\itshape c\+Rows} should equal {\itshape CRows} unless {\itshape CRows} is {\itshape Dynamic}, and the same for the number of columns.

The parameters {\itshape rows} and {\itshape cols} are the number of rows and of columns of the returned matrix. Must be compatible with this \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base}{Dense\+Base} type.

This variant is meant to be used for dynamic-\/size matrix types. For fixed-\/size types, it is redundant to pass {\itshape rows} and {\itshape cols} as arguments, so \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a8c4be762b10041d64a2b2ce85bb14ba0_a8c4be762b10041d64a2b2ce85bb14ba0}{Zero()} should be used instead.

The template parameter {\itshape Custom\+Nullary\+Op} is the type of the functor.

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

 Output\+: 
\begin{DoxyVerbInclude}
\end{DoxyVerbInclude}


The number of coefficients {\itshape n} can also be specified at compile-\/time by passing \doxylink{namespace_eigen_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418}{Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$}, or Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$(n) as arguments. See \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_aea3e98d95cd47acce1bf5721f573ed70_aea3e98d95cd47acce1bf5721f573ed70}{block() } for the details.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
Even in the case that the returned expression has dynamic size, in the case when it is applied to a fixed-\/size vector, it inherits a fixed maximal size, which means that evaluating it does not cause a dynamic memory allocation.
\end{DoxyNote}

the inner size.
\end{DoxyReturn}
\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
For a vector, this is just the size. For a matrix (non-\/vector), this is the minor dimension with respect to the \doxylink{group___topic_storage_orders}{storage order}, i.\+e., the number of rows for a column-\/major matrix, and the number of columns for a row-\/major matrix. 
\end{DoxyNote}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_af3793521d788a946cb9c5f4f92bab4d3_af3793521d788a946cb9c5f4f92bab4d3} 
template$<$typename Derived $>$ \\
template$<$typename Other\+Derived $>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} bool \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base}{Eigen\+::\+Dense\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::is\+Approx (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base}{Dense\+Base}}$<$ Other\+Derived $>$ \&}]{other}{, }\item[{const \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a52eccadb19e2182ce3530b7d6db22c6c_a52eccadb19e2182ce3530b7d6db22c6c}{Real\+Scalar}} \&}]{prec}{ = {\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{struct_eigen_1_1_num_traits}{Num\+Traits}}$<$\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a4116dfe923b430cc38d03b83d0bca985_a4116dfe923b430cc38d03b83d0bca985}{Scalar}}$>$\+:\+:dummy\+\_\+precision()}}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
{\ttfamily true} if {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} is approximately equal to {\itshape other}, within the precision determined by {\itshape prec}.
\end{DoxyReturn}
\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
The fuzzy compares are done multiplicatively. Two vectors $ v $ and $ w $ are considered to be approximately equal within precision $ p $ if \[ \Vert v - w \Vert \leqslant p\,\min(\Vert v\Vert, \Vert w\Vert). \] For matrices, the comparison is done using the Hilbert-\/\+Schmidt norm (aka Frobenius norm L2 norm).

Because of the multiplicativeness of this comparison, one can\textquotesingle{}t use this function to check whether {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} is approximately equal to the zero matrix or vector. Indeed, {\ttfamily is\+Approx(zero)} returns false unless {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} itself is exactly the zero matrix or vector. If you want to test whether {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} is zero, use internal\+::is\+Much\+Smaller\+Than(const \+Real\+Scalar\&, Real\+Scalar) instead.
\end{DoxyNote}
\begin{DoxySeeAlso}{参照}
internal\+::is\+Much\+Smaller\+Than(const Real\+Scalar\&, Real\+Scalar) const 
\end{DoxySeeAlso}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
{\ttfamily true} if the norm of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} is much smaller than the norm of {\itshape other}, within the precision determined by {\itshape prec}.
\end{DoxyReturn}
\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
The fuzzy compares are done multiplicatively. A vector $ v $ is considered to be much smaller than a vector $ w $ within precision $ p $ if \[ \Vert v \Vert \leqslant p\,\Vert w\Vert. \] For matrices, the comparison is done using the Hilbert-\/\+Schmidt norm.
\end{DoxyNote}

\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} bool \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base}{Eigen\+::\+Dense\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::is\+Much\+Smaller\+Than (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const typename \mbox{\hyperlink{struct_eigen_1_1_num_traits}{Num\+Traits}}$<$ \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a4116dfe923b430cc38d03b83d0bca985_a4116dfe923b430cc38d03b83d0bca985}{Scalar}} $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{boostmultiprec_8cpp_ad5abe8020ead32dcab5e3c15d6d7c30e_ad5abe8020ead32dcab5e3c15d6d7c30e}{\+::\+Real}} \&}]{other}{, }\item[{const \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a52eccadb19e2182ce3530b7d6db22c6c_a52eccadb19e2182ce3530b7d6db22c6c}{Real\+Scalar}} \&}]{prec}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
{\ttfamily true} if the norm of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} is much smaller than {\itshape other}, within the precision determined by {\itshape prec}.
\end{DoxyReturn}
\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
The fuzzy compares are done multiplicatively. A vector $ v $ is considered to be much smaller than $ x $ within precision $ p $ if \[ \Vert v \Vert \leqslant p\,\vert x\vert. \]
\end{DoxyNote}
For matrices, the comparison is done using the Hilbert-\/\+Schmidt norm. For this reason, the value of the reference scalar {\itshape other} should come from the Hilbert-\/\+Schmidt norm of a reference matrix of same dimensions.

\end{DoxySeeAlso}

Sets a linearly spaced vector.    

The function generates \textquotesingle{}size\textquotesingle{} equally spaced values in the closed interval \mbox{[}low,high\mbox{]}. When size is set to 1, a vector of length 1 containing \textquotesingle{}high\textquotesingle{} is returned.

\textbackslash{}only\+\_\+for\+\_\+vectors

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+: 
\begin{DoxyVerbInclude}
\end{DoxyVerbInclude}


For integer scalar types, an even spacing is possible if and only if the length of the range, i.\+e., {\ttfamily high-\/low} is a scalar multiple of {\ttfamily size-\/1}, or if {\ttfamily size} is a scalar multiple of the number of values {\ttfamily high-\/low+1} (meaning each value can be repeated the same number of time). If one of these two considions is not satisfied, then {\ttfamily high} is lowered to the largest value satisfying one of this constraint. Here are some examples\+:

Example\+:

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}

\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_af2b60117c00a6e75812de43bfe7db3b1_af2b60117c00a6e75812de43bfe7db3b1}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+STRONG\+\_\+\+INLINE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{struct_eigen_1_1internal_1_1traits}{internal\+::traits}}$<$ Derived $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9}{\+::\+Scalar}} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base}{Eigen\+::\+Dense\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::max\+Coeff (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
the maximum of all coefficients of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this}. In case {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} contains NaN, Na\+NPropagation determines the behavior\+: Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+Fast \+: undefined Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+NaN \+: result is NaN Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+Numbers \+: result is maximum of elements that are not NaN 
\end{DoxyReturn}
\begin{DoxyWarning}{警告}
the matrix must be not empty, otherwise an assertion is triggered.

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
the maximum of all coefficients of \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this and puts in \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}index its location.
\end{DoxyReturn}
In case {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} contains NaN, Na\+NPropagation determines the behavior\+: Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+Fast \+: undefined Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+NaN \+: result is NaN Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+Numbers \+: result is maximum of elements that are not NaN \begin{DoxyWarning}{警告}
the matrix must be not empty, otherwise an assertion is triggered.
\end{DoxyWarning}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
the minimum of all coefficients of {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this}. In case {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} contains NaN, Na\+NPropagation determines the behavior\+: Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+Fast \+: undefined Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+NaN \+: result is NaN Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+Numbers \+: result is minimum of elements that are not NaN 
\end{DoxyReturn}
\begin{DoxyWarning}{警告}
the matrix must be not empty, otherwise an assertion is triggered. 
\end{DoxyWarning}

In case {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} contains NaN, Na\+NPropagation determines the behavior\+: Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+Fast \+: undefined Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+NaN \+: result is NaN Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+Numbers \+: result is maximum of elements that are not NaN \begin{DoxyWarning}{警告}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
the minimum of all coefficients of \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this and puts in \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}row and \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}col its location.
\end{DoxyReturn}
In case {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} contains NaN, Na\+NPropagation determines the behavior\+: Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+Fast \+: undefined Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+NaN \+: result is NaN Na\+NPropagation == Propagate\+Numbers \+: result is maximum of elements that are not NaN \begin{DoxyWarning}{警告}
the matrix must be not empty, otherwise an assertion is triggered.
\end{DoxyWarning}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
an expression of a matrix defined by a custom functor {\itshape func} 
\end{DoxyReturn}
The parameters {\itshape rows} and {\itshape cols} are the number of rows and of columns of the returned matrix. Must be compatible with this \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base} type.

This variant is meant to be used for dynamic-\/size matrix types. For fixed-\/size types, it is redundant to pass {\itshape rows} and {\itshape cols} as arguments, so \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a8c4be762b10041d64a2b2ce85bb14ba0_a8c4be762b10041d64a2b2ce85bb14ba0}{Zero()} should be used instead.

The template parameter {\itshape Custom\+Nullary\+Op} is the type of the functor.

The parameter {\itshape size} is the size of the returned vector. Must be compatible with this \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base} type.

\textbackslash{}only\+\_\+for\+\_\+vectors

This variant is meant to be used for dynamic-\/size vector types. For fixed-\/size types, it is redundant to pass {\itshape size} as argument, so \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a8c4be762b10041d64a2b2ce85bb14ba0_a8c4be762b10041d64a2b2ce85bb14ba0}{Zero()} should be used instead.

The template parameter {\itshape Custom\+Nullary\+Op} is the type of the functor.

an expression of a matrix where all coefficients equal one.
\end{DoxyReturn}
The parameters {\itshape rows} and {\itshape cols} are the number of rows and of columns of the returned matrix. Must be compatible with this \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base} type.

This variant is meant to be used for dynamic-\/size matrix types. For fixed-\/size types, it is redundant to pass {\itshape rows} and {\itshape cols} as arguments, so \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a1284a38971d817c33e40c226f6347e37_a1284a38971d817c33e40c226f6347e37}{Ones()} should be used instead.

Convenient operator to set the coefficients of a matrix.

The coefficients must be provided in a row major order and exactly match the size of the matrix. Otherwise an assertion is raised.

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+: 
\begin{DoxyVerbInclude}
\end{DoxyVerbInclude}


\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
According the c++ standard, the argument expressions of this comma initializer are evaluated in arbitrary order.

Copies the generic expression {\itshape other} into \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this. 

The expression must provide a (templated) eval\+To(\+Derived\& dst) const function which does the actual job. In practice, this allows any user to write its own special matrix without having to modify \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base}

a reference to \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this. 
\end{DoxyReturn}

\end{DoxyReturn}
\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
For a vector, this returns just 1. For a matrix (non-\/vector), this is the major dimension with respect to the \doxylink{group___topic_storage_orders}{storage order}, i.\+e., the number of columns for a column-\/major matrix, and the number of rows for a row-\/major matrix. 
\end{DoxyNote}

a fixed-\/size random matrix or vector expression
\end{DoxyReturn}
Numbers are uniformly spread through their whole definition range for integer types, and in the \mbox{[}-\/1\+:1\mbox{]} range for floating point scalar types.

This variant is only for fixed-\/size \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base} types. For dynamic-\/size types, you need to use the variants taking size arguments.

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+: 
\begin{DoxyVerbInclude}
\end{DoxyVerbInclude}


This expression has the "{}evaluate before nesting"{} flag so that it will be evaluated into a temporary matrix whenever it is nested in a larger expression. This prevents unexpected behavior with expressions involving random matrices.

\textbackslash{}not\+\_\+reentrant

const \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base}{Dense\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a5ec93c0d53ffa42ebc219bd96c524f9c_a5ec93c0d53ffa42ebc219bd96c524f9c}{\+::\+Random\+Return\+Type}} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base}{Eigen\+::\+Dense\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::\+Random (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{namespace_eigen_a62e77e0933482dafde8fe197d9a2cfde_a62e77e0933482dafde8fe197d9a2cfde}{Index}}}]{rows}{, }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{namespace_eigen_a62e77e0933482dafde8fe197d9a2cfde_a62e77e0933482dafde8fe197d9a2cfde}{Index}}}]{cols}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
a random matrix expression
\end{DoxyReturn}
Numbers are uniformly spread through their whole definition range for integer types, and in the \mbox{[}-\/1\+:1\mbox{]} range for floating point scalar types.

The parameters {\itshape rows} and {\itshape cols} are the number of rows and of columns of the returned matrix. Must be compatible with this \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base} type.

\textbackslash{}not\+\_\+reentrant

This variant is meant to be used for dynamic-\/size matrix types. For fixed-\/size types, it is redundant to pass {\itshape rows} and {\itshape cols} as arguments, so \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_ae814abb451b48ed872819192dc188c19_ae814abb451b48ed872819192dc188c19}{Random()} should be used instead.

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+: 
\begin{DoxyVerbInclude}
\end{DoxyVerbInclude}


This expression has the "{}evaluate before nesting"{} flag so that it will be evaluated into a temporary matrix whenever it is nested in a larger expression. This prevents unexpected behavior with expressions involving random matrices.

See \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a08224b09985b372f108850b1c605feed_a08224b09985b372f108850b1c605feed}{Dense\+Base\+::\+Nullary\+Expr(\+Index, const Custom\+Nullary\+Op\&)} for an example using C++11 random generators.

Numbers are uniformly spread through their whole definition range for integer types, and in the \mbox{[}-\/1\+:1\mbox{]} range for floating point scalar types.

The parameter {\itshape size} is the size of the returned vector. Must be compatible with this \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_matrix_base}{Matrix\+Base} type.

\textbackslash{}only\+\_\+for\+\_\+vectors \textbackslash{}not\+\_\+reentrant

\begin{DoxyReturn}{戻り値}
the result of a full redux operation on the whole matrix or vector using {\itshape func} 
\end{DoxyReturn}
The template parameter {\itshape Binary\+Op} is the type of the functor {\itshape func} which must be an associative operator. Both current C++98 and C++11 functor styles are handled.

\begin{DoxyWarning}{警告}
the matrix must be not empty, otherwise an assertion is triggered.
\end{DoxyWarning}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{reverseInPlace()}{reverseInPlace()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a11014cd4565501e332bdd38c0028f736_a11014cd4565501e332bdd38c0028f736} 
template$<$typename Derived $>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} void \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base}{Eigen\+::\+Dense\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::reverse\+In\+Place (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}

This is the "{}in place"{} version of reverse\+: it reverses {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this}.

In most cases it is probably better to simply use the reversed expression of a matrix. However, when reversing the matrix data itself is really needed, then this "{}in-\/place"{} version is probably the right choice because it provides the following additional benefits\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item less error prone\+: doing the same operation with .\doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_acde1eef3a99bbbb4ac694ca55b1764c8_acde1eef3a99bbbb4ac694ca55b1764c8}{reverse()} requires special care\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}\ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.reverse().eval();\ }

\end{DoxyCode}

\item this API enables reverse operations without the need for a temporary
\item it allows future optimizations (cache friendliness, etc.)
\end{DoxyItemize}

\begin{DoxySeeAlso}{参照}
\doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_vectorwise_op_a2bd419527acce58e9e813a5af654b81c_a2bd419527acce58e9e813a5af654b81c}{Vectorwise\+Op\+::reverse\+In\+Place()}, \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_acde1eef3a99bbbb4ac694ca55b1764c8_acde1eef3a99bbbb4ac694ca55b1764c8}{reverse()} 
\end{DoxySeeAlso}

The number of coefficients {\itshape n} can also be specified at compile-\/time by passing \doxylink{namespace_eigen_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418}{Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$}, or Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$(n) as arguments. See \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_aea3e98d95cd47acce1bf5721f573ed70_aea3e98d95cd47acce1bf5721f573ed70}{block() } for the details.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
Even in the case that the returned expression has dynamic size, in the case when it is applied to a fixed-\/size vector, it inherits a fixed maximal size, which means that evaluating it does not cause a dynamic memory allocation.
\end{DoxyNote}

Sets a linearly spaced vector. 

The function fills {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} with equally spaced values in the closed interval \mbox{[}low,high\mbox{]}. When size is set to 1, a vector of length 1 containing \textquotesingle{}high\textquotesingle{} is returned.

\textbackslash{}only\+\_\+for\+\_\+vectors

For integer scalar types, do not miss the explanations on the definition of \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a513c7986f48517c36f992a558f81e591_a513c7986f48517c36f992a558f81e591}{even spacing }.

The number of coefficients {\itshape n} can also be specified at compile-\/time by passing \doxylink{namespace_eigen_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418_a8636241003a5d035f1832765ba8e5418}{Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$}, or Eigen\+::fix$<$\+N$>$(n) as arguments. See \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_aea3e98d95cd47acce1bf5721f573ed70_aea3e98d95cd47acce1bf5721f573ed70}{block() } for the details.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
Even in the case that the returned expression has dynamic size, in the case when it is applied to a fixed-\/size vector, it inherits a fixed maximal size, which means that evaluating it does not cause a dynamic memory allocation.
\end{DoxyNote}

This function is mainly useful for corners where the number of rows is specified at compile-\/time and the number of columns is specified at run-\/time, or vice versa. The compile-\/time and run-\/time information should not contradict. In other words, {\itshape c\+Rows} should equal {\itshape CRows} unless {\itshape CRows} is {\itshape Dynamic}, and the same for the number of columns.

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+:

This function is mainly useful for corners where the number of rows is specified at compile-\/time and the number of columns is specified at run-\/time, or vice versa. The compile-\/time and run-\/time information should not contradict. In other words, {\itshape c\+Rows} should equal {\itshape CRows} unless {\itshape CRows} is {\itshape Dynamic}, and the same for the number of columns.

Example\+: 
\begin{DoxyCodeInclude}{0}

\end{DoxyCodeInclude}
 Output\+:

\end{DoxyVerbInclude}

\begin{DoxyWarning}{警告}
If you want to replace a matrix by its own transpose, do {\bfseries{NOT}} do this\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}\ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.transpose();\ \textcolor{comment}{//\ bug!!!\ caused\ by\ aliasing\ effect}}

\end{DoxyCode}
 Instead, use the \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a2bf1856f4ff79f0db0e6d416a2edfb0f_a2bf1856f4ff79f0db0e6d416a2edfb0f}{transpose\+In\+Place()} method\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.transposeInPlace();}

\end{DoxyCode}
 which gives \doxylink{namespace_eigen}{Eigen} good opportunities for optimization, or alternatively you can also do\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}\ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.transpose().eval();}

\end{DoxyCode}

\end{DoxyWarning}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a2bf1856f4ff79f0db0e6d416a2edfb0f_a2bf1856f4ff79f0db0e6d416a2edfb0f} 
template$<$typename Derived $>$ \\
\mbox{\hyperlink{_macros_8h_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da_a9efec3cfd22b9a33bead9c6718d128da}{EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEVICE\+\_\+\+FUNC}} void \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base}{Eigen\+::\+Dense\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$\+::transpose\+In\+Place (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}

This is the "{}in place"{} version of \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_af891b9782f712151a471b3d7cfaaa2e8_af891b9782f712151a471b3d7cfaaa2e8}{transpose()}\+: it replaces {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} by its own transpose. Thus, doing 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.transposeInPlace();}

\end{DoxyCode}
 has the same effect on m as doing 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}\ =\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_angle_axis__mimic__euler_8cpp_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a_ab3cd915d758008bd19d0f2428fbb354a}{m}}.transpose().eval();}

\end{DoxyCode}
 and is faster and also safer because in the latter line of code, forgetting the \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a5df64c66228ba75bbc66db2584185527_a5df64c66228ba75bbc66db2584185527}{eval()} results in a bug caused by \doxylink{group___topic_aliasing}{aliasing}.

Notice however that this method is only useful if you want to replace a matrix by its own transpose. If you just need the transpose of a matrix, use \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_af891b9782f712151a471b3d7cfaaa2e8_af891b9782f712151a471b3d7cfaaa2e8}{transpose()}.

\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
if the matrix is not square, then {\ttfamily \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}this} must be a resizable matrix. This excludes (non-\/square) fixed-\/size matrices, block-\/expressions and maps.
\end{DoxyNote}

\end{DoxySeeAlso}

Applies the visitor {\itshape visitor} to the whole coefficients of the matrix or vector.

The template parameter {\itshape Visitor} is the type of the visitor and provides the following interface\+: 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\textcolor{keyword}{struct\ }MyVisitor\ \{}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \textcolor{comment}{//\ called\ for\ the\ first\ coefficient}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \textcolor{keywordtype}{void}\ \mbox{\hyperlink{smooth__opengl3_8c_a2858154e2009b0e6e616f313177762bc_a2858154e2009b0e6e616f313177762bc}{init}}(\textcolor{keyword}{const}\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9}{Scalar}}\&\ value,\ Index\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_bi_c_g_s_t_a_b__step__by__step_8cpp_acb559820d9ca11295b4500f179ef6392_acb559820d9ca11295b4500f179ef6392}{i}},\ Index\ \mbox{\hyperlink{tut__arithmetic__redux__minmax_8cpp_a39513f0f8d942bf9ef17b2e87ac6d34e_a39513f0f8d942bf9ef17b2e87ac6d34e}{j}});}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \textcolor{comment}{//\ called\ for\ all\ other\ coefficients}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \textcolor{keywordtype}{void}\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_indexed_view_methods_8h_a745b5bbf26fefb72e16675ef6ccd9057_a745b5bbf26fefb72e16675ef6ccd9057}{operator()\ }}(\textcolor{keyword}{const}\ \mbox{\hyperlink{bench__gemm_8cpp_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9_a052eb942d12b6404aade6fae4b075fb9}{Scalar}}\&\ value,\ Index\ \mbox{\hyperlink{_bi_c_g_s_t_a_b__step__by__step_8cpp_acb559820d9ca11295b4500f179ef6392_acb559820d9ca11295b4500f179ef6392}{i}},\ Index\ \mbox{\hyperlink{tut__arithmetic__redux__minmax_8cpp_a39513f0f8d942bf9ef17b2e87ac6d34e_a39513f0f8d942bf9ef17b2e87ac6d34e}{j}});}
\DoxyCodeLine{\};}

\end{DoxyCode}


\begin{DoxyNote}{覚え書き}
compared to one or two {\itshape for} {\itshape loops}, visitors offer automatic unrolling for small fixed size matrix.

if the matrix is empty, then the visitor is left unchanged.
\end{DoxyNote}

std\+::ostream \& operator$<$$<$ (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{std\+::ostream \&}]{s}{, }\item[{const \mbox{\hyperlink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base}{Dense\+Base}}$<$ Derived $>$ \&}]{m}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [related]}}

Outputs the matrix, to the given stream.

If you wish to print the matrix with a format different than the default, use \doxylink{class_eigen_1_1_dense_base_a148e20e32aa3ceca13e0a49c2cc584fc_a148e20e32aa3ceca13e0a49c2cc584fc}{Dense\+Base\+::format()}.

It is also possible to change the default format by defining EIGEN\+\_\+\+DEFAULT\+\_\+\+IO\+\_\+\+FORMAT before including \doxylink{namespace_eigen}{Eigen} headers. If not defined, this will automatically be defined to \doxylink{struct_eigen_1_1_i_o_format}{Eigen\+::\+IOFormat()}, that is the \doxylink{struct_eigen_1_1_i_o_format}{Eigen\+::\+IOFormat} with default parameters.
